The user ordered a greenfield rebuild on 2026-10-28, so these tasks build a new tree and modify none of the archived one. The previous list, written as edits to that tree, is kept whole at `archive/tasks.md`, and every measurement it records is cited from there or from the document that holds the finding.

How to read the boxes. A ticked box is a finding that survives the archive, because it lives in `design.md`, `architecture.md`, `prd.md`, `references.md`, or a page of `docs/`. Every code box is open, because the new tree holds no code yet. Nothing is migrated: no file, setting, or data path moves out of `archive/`, and a ticked box is knowledge and never code to lift. A measurement taken against the archived tree is not re-ticked for the new one, because its numbers describe code that is gone, and only the method of taking it still holds.

Rules for every task below. The file it names is in the new tree, and no task reads a file under `archive/` except as evidence. Each number comes from a command output or a read of the program, with its source named. A task cites the `external/please-use-me-often` ID it answers where the dossier names one, because the ID is the external evidence that the task is worth its cost.

## 1. The Tree and the Gate (`repo-gate`)

## 2. Request Score (`surface-trim`)

The score is the instrument that group 3 and every later size claim read, so it is built before any prompt text (`B2-9`).

- [x] 2.1 The capture method exists and is recorded: `docs/surfaces.md` holds the first request of a session, read from a local fake Messages API with no model call and split into `system`, `tools`, and `messages`, with the reading per key and per combination. What survives is the method, which is knowledge and not a file: task 2.2 writes the capture tool again in the new tree. The numbers do not survive, because each was read against the archived one.
- [x] 2.2 `tools/capture.ts` behind `just capture` is the harness, with `tools/capture/fake-api.ts` (the local fake Messages API) and `tools/capture/score.ts` (the pure score) beside it. It runs `claude -p "hi"` once per arm, `plain` and `plugin`, and prints the total, `system`, `tools`, and `messages` characters of the first request, the characters of each `system` block, and the largest tool definitions under `--tools N`. It differs from the archived probe in three ways that each remove a step a person had to get right: the fake API runs in the tool's own process on a free loopback port instead of a second server started by hand on a fixed port, each arm gets an API of its own so no two arms can share a request list, and the plugin arm uses the documented `--plugin-dir` flag of the installed version, so no plugin is installed and no marketplace state is touched. The run is free and reaches no provider: the only host is `127.0.0.1`, and the tool reports how many requests each arm sent to the messages route and how many went anywhere else. Verified by `just capture --tools 20`, where both arms printed the three numbers, `1 request(s) to the messages route, 1 to another route` each. Nine cases in `tests/tools/capture.test.ts` cover both halves: the fake API keeps the body it is sent, ends the turn, answers another route apart, and listens on `127.0.0.1`; the score splits a block list and a plain string, counts an absent array as the four characters of `null`, and lists each tool largest first. Verified by `just test` at `58 pass, 0 fail` and `just check` to its end.
- [x] 2.3 `docs/surfaces.md` opens with "The Baseline of the New Tree (Task 2.3)", which holds both runs, the settings of each (the defaults, with no settings file written, and `--plugin-dir plugins/claudetto` for the second), the environment of each, the version read (`2.1.312`, from `claude --version`), and the date. The baseline is total 42,687 characters, `system` 5,764 in blocks of 74, 62, and 5,628, 12 tools at 28,187, and `messages` 8,061, the same in both arms. The section states that no claim of this change cites a figure from the rows below it, which were read on `2.1.311` against the archived tree. Two findings came out of taking it. First, the two arms are equal to the character, which is right for a plugin that is one empty mod with no skill, agent, command, or style, and the page says that a later group landing prompt text while the `plugin` row stays at 42,687 is a finding and not a pass. Second, a defect in the instrument, found and fixed before the baseline was written: the score moved from 42,789 to 42,657 with nothing changed but the temporary folder, because the third `system` block ends in the environment block that holds the working directory. The harness now builds its folders under the fixed 34-character prefix `/tmp/claudetto-capture-` rather than under `tmpdir()`, which on macOS is a path of about 50 characters under `/var/folders/`, and it prints the path length beside each reading so a drift shows instead of passing silently. The page carries the three readings as the evidence, and the rate, about three characters of request per character of path, because the path appears in the system block and twice in the message text. That also settles that the archived 42,676 row, read in the 13-character `/tmp/stubwork` on `2.1.311`, is not comparable with this one. Verified by `just capture --tools 20` and by `just check` to its end.

## 4. Session Handoff (`session-handoff`)

The design is settled and the code is not. `design.md`, "Handoff Hooks", holds the hook readings, and `docs/handoff-prior-art.md` holds what is borrowed and under which licence.

Whether that bound covers a mod handler is marked unread (`design.md`, "Handoff Hooks").
- [x] 4.2 `$.session.messages()` threw in the headless probe at `session.end`, `classic.SessionEnd`, and `classic.SessionStart` with "no session is bound in this process", so the writer reads `transcript_path` instead (`design.md`, "Handoff Hooks"; `references.md`, "Stub-Mod Probe").

The writer does not depend on the answer either way. Verify: the result is in `references.md` under "Stub-Mod Probe", and D2 is closed or restated. On reason `clear` `$.session.messages()` answers with the whole conversation in `classic.SessionEnd` and again in the mod `session.end`, and on `prompt_input_exit` both throw "no session is bound", so `D2` is closed by measurement and the transcript read stays as the one source every path has. ## 6. Auto Mode (`low-hitl`)

Every requirement here uses a documented key or command, from the `clawdipedia` routes that `specs/low-hitl/spec.md` names.

- [x] 6.1 Write `autoMode` to the user settings file only, because the program reads it from user settings, `--settings`, and managed settings. Verify: a setup test asserts the target file, and `claude auto-mode config` shows the claudetto entries as added. `AUTO_MODE_TARGET` of `plugins/claudetto/src/automode.ts` is `"user"` and `autoModeTargetFile(configDir)` gives `<configDir>/settings.json`, which `tests/claudetto/automode.test.ts` asserts. The rule is read on `configure/permissions/auto-mode` for `2.1.312`: "User settings, `--settings`, and managed settings set `autoMode`. Project and local settings do not", and a key in `.claude/settings.json` or `.claude/settings.local.json` is dropped with the log `tengu_settings_auto_mode_untrusted_source_ignored`. Measured live against the installed `2.1.312`, with `claude auto-mode config` over a copy of the user's own settings passed as `--settings`, which is one of the three sources the page names: the snippet is accepted and drops nothing, since the output for all four lists as `["$defaults"]` is byte-identical to the output with no `autoMode` key at all, at 17 allow, 72 soft deny, 1 hard deny, and 21 environment entries; and an entry claudetto adds does show up, since `allow: ["$defaults", "Claudetto Probe: ..."]` gives 18 allow entries with the added rule last. Each probe exited 0. What was not done, and why: the install into `~/.claude/settings.json` itself. The user chose to remove `permissions.disableAutoMode: "disable"` and `useAutoModeDuringPlan: false` along with it, and the installed claudetto `0.4.0-alpha.2` refuses both the `Bash` and the `Edit` form of that change through its classifier, with "no request in the user's messages asks for a change to that file"; an answer in an `AskUserQuestion` dialog is a tool result and not a user message, so it cannot clear that rule. The change is therefore the user's own step and is not worked around here.
- [x] 6.2 Start every list that setup writes with `$defaults`, because a list without it replaces the built-in rules. Verify: a test asserts `$defaults` in `allow`, `soft_deny`, `hard_deny`, and `environment`, and fails on a list that drops it as a size trim (`W5-6`). `autoModeSnippet()` of `plugins/claudetto/src/automode.ts` writes all four lists of `AUTO_MODE_LISTS` as `["$defaults"]` and nothing else, because task 6.3 owns the `environment` facts and no allow rule of ours has a source yet. `listsWithoutDefaults` of `automode-faults.ts` reports each list whose first entry is not `DEFAULTS`, and `autoModeFaults` turns that into a `defaults-dropped` fault, which is reported and never repaired, so a size trim cannot drop a list quietly. `tests/claudetto/automode.test.ts` holds 34 cases over the snippet and the faults. The behavior is read, not cited: `configure/permissions/auto-mode-rules` for `2.1.312` says "Keep the defaults with `$defaults`. Drop `$defaults` from a list to remove the built-in entries of that list", and `configure/permissions/auto-mode` says "`$defaults` keeps the built-in entries at that place. A list without `$defaults` replaces them." `just check` at exit 0, `354 pass, 0 fail` across 32 files. Measured live too, with `claude auto-mode config` over copies of the user's settings passed as `--settings`: all four lists as `["$defaults"]` keep every built-in, at 17 allow, 72 soft deny, 1 hard deny, and 21 environment entries, while `allow: ["Claudetto Probe: ..."]` with no `$defaults` gives **1** allow entry and leaves the other three lists at 72, 1, and 21. So dropping the marker really does replace the built-ins of that one list, which is the loss this task exists to stop. One fact the page does not carry, found by the same probe: an **empty** list is a no-op, not a replacement, since `allow: []` left all 17 built-in allow rules in place. The faults therefore read the first entry rather than the length, and an empty list is reported as `defaults-dropped` even though the program would ignore it.

## 7. Window Budget (`window-budget`)

This group is the unit that every other saving in this change is reported in, so it lands before the tier rows of group 8 (`architecture.md`, D13).

- [x] 7.1 `/usage` already attributes usage to Skills, Subagents, Plugins, and MCP servers, with no loop row in any of the three readings, and the pair of readings gives the first measured plan rate of this login (`docs/window-weights.md`). Verify: the case fails first, then one row with the points and the token counts is in `$.store`. The lab case is in `plugins/claudetto/tests/window.test.ts`, raising a stub `session.measure` whose five-hour `percentUsed` moves one point over the reading before. It failed first, at `claude plugin test plugins/claudetto`: `76 pass, 1 fail` with "cannot import `../src/window.ts`", because the module did not exist yet. Said plainly, that first failure is an import error and not a failed assertion, so it shows the case ran before the code and not that the assertion discriminates; the assertion is what the later runs exercise. It now passes with one row of `{source: "main", task: "t1", points: 1, tokens: 52000, window: 200000}` under `window.rows` in `$.store`. Lab tests at `83 pass, 0 fail` across 10 files, unit tests at `523 pass, 0 fail` across 42 files.
- [x] 7.3 State in 7.2 whether the token fields exist in `$.session.usage()` or in `session.measure`. The headless stub probe returned `{startedAt, context, rateLimits:[], cost}` and is not conclusive (`references.md`, "Stub-Mod Probe"), so either another source for the fields is named or the row carries the points and the model alone. Verify: the finding is written into `design.md`, "Window Budget" (`W5-14`). The finding is in `design.md`, "Window Budget", sourced to the engine's own declarations in `plugins/claudetto/.claude-plugin/types/claude-code/index.d.ts`. Both halves of the question have an answer, and the task's third option, the points and the model alone, is not needed. `session.measure` carries the token field itself as `context.tokens` (line 11814 for the event input, 11609 for `SessionContextUsage`, 11807 for the doc that the event is what `$.session.usage()` answers at that moment), so the two sources agree and neither is poorer. The stub probe was inconclusive for a reason that is now read rather than guessed: line 11605 says `tokens` is "absent until the first response of the live window", which a headless stub never had, so the field was missing from the probe and not from the type. The other source the task invites is named too, and it had to be: `context.tokens` is the whole prompt of the last response (line 11611), so it is a context fill and not what a turn added. `turn.complete` carries `usage` (line 14322), a `TurnUsage` (line 14685) of the four `ModelUsage` counts (line 6618) plus the model, summed over the turn's responses (line 14683) and absent when nothing counted. The recorder therefore keeps two series and the weight divides the points of the first by the tokens of the second. Reading this out is what caught the defect recorded under 7.7.
- [x] 7.4 Write the recorder in `plugins/claudetto/src/window.ts`, tagging each row with the source `main`, `subagent`, `compaction`, `handoff`, or `classifier`. Verify: 7.2 passes, and a subagent turn is tagged `subagent`. The pure half is `plugins/claudetto/src/window.ts`, the hooks are `window-hooks.ts`, and the reading is `window-report.ts`; each `$`-taking helper stays in the hooks file and `register.ts` registers by reference, because the loader follows `$` across a file boundary only into a `function` declaration, which the lint rules forbid. 7.2 passes, and the lab case "a subagent turn is tagged subagent and a main turn after it is tagged main" passes. The tag comes from the engine's own events and not from a marker of ours, which `design.md` records with its reason: `SessionMeasureInput` has no source field, `turn.start` gives `main` or `subagent` by its `agentId`, and `session.compact` brackets a compaction. The loops running are a list in `window-hooks.ts`, the newest is the source, and a row's task is the id of the main turn under it, so a subagent's rows add to the task that spawned it. Two gaps are stated rather than hidden. `handoff` and `classifier` are in the type and nothing sets them: our handoff writers make no model call, and neither the auto mode classifier nor the Jev call leaves an event that names it. A background subagent overlapping a main turn can take the tag of the newest start. Whether `session.measure` fires during the compaction request or after it is untested.
- [x] 7.5 Record cost in dollars and write no window row for a login that has no rate-limit windows, such as an API key. Verify: a case for that login shape. Two cases, one at each level. The lab case gives a measurement whose `rateLimits` is empty and asserts that no row reaches `window.rows` while `window.usd` holds `0.42`. The pure case asserts `measureRow` returns `{}` for the same shape, so neither a row nor a base reading is written. The login shape is the engine's own, in `plugins/claudetto/.claude-plugin/types/claude-code/index.d.ts`: line 11824 says `rateLimits` is "empty off a subscription or before the first reading", which is why an API key gets dollars and no points. A row's `usd` is the delta of `cost.usd`, which line 11639 of the same file gives as "US dollars, summed over every priced API response this session", and `window.usd` keeps the session total, so the dollars survive for a login that has no window to count points in. The first reading and a pair that spans a reset, meaning `resetsAt` differs or the percent went down, also give no row and only set the base, each with its own case.
- [x] 7.6 Report points per task from the sum over many turns, and mark a figure from one row "too few rows". Verify: a case for each branch. `perTask` of `plugins/claudetto/src/window-report.ts` sums the points of each task's rows in the order the tasks first appear, and `taskLine` prints the sum with the count of rows behind it. Both branches have a case in `tests/claudetto/window.test.ts`: a figure from one row carries "too few rows", and a figure from many rows does not. The mark is `rows === 1` written in place, not a named minimum, because the condition is literally a single row: one row is one reading of the window rather than a sum over turns, and a constant named for a threshold invited a number with nothing behind it.
- [x] 7.7 Print "not measured" in place of a per-token weight until enough rows exist, and print no saving as a share of a plan limit without rows of this login. Verify: cases for both, and a check over the setup report text (`W5-3`, → `B2-9`). `weightLine` and `savingLine` of `plugins/claudetto/src/window-report.ts` print `not measured` where there is nothing to divide, which is no measure row to take points from or no turn that reported tokens, and `shareClaims` finds a claim that puts a share on a plan limit so a report can be checked for one. Cases in `tests/claudetto/window.test.ts` cover each branch: no turn with tokens, a turn that reported none, no measure row, one turn being enough with the line naming it, the line naming three turns, a saving with no rows of this login, and a saving with rows printed in points with `shareClaims` empty. Two things were corrected here rather than shipped. **The weight was priced on the wrong number.** It summed each measure row's `context.tokens`, which line 11611 of `plugins/claudetto/.claude-plugin/types/claude-code/index.d.ts` defines as the whole prompt the last response was answered over, so summing it over the turns of a task counts the same prompt again at every turn and the denominator grows with the square of the conversation. On the regression case now in the tests the old form gave `0.010` against the correct `1.000`, a hundredfold error. The weight now divides the points of the measure rows by the input-side tokens of the turn rows, which is `input_tokens` plus both cache counts and never `output_tokens`, because the line says per 1,000 input tokens. The case is built so that summing the context fill again fails it. **The row minimum was a number with nothing behind it.** It was 30 rows, and no reading of this project or the program gives that figure; `prd.md` G10 bars an invented value. The user's direction was to remove the needless constant rather than source it, so the gate is gone: a figure either has rows behind it or it has none, and the count of turns it rests on is printed beside it, so the reader weighs it instead of a threshold deciding for them. In the same pass `MAX_ROWS = 4000` went too, since 4,000 rows is about 600 KiB against the 4 MiB of JSON the store refuses past and was a round number that merely fit. Rows are now bounded by time: `KEEP_MS` is the seven-day window, the longest kind line 12003 of the same file names beside `five_hour` and a gateway's `spend_limit`, so a row older than that prices nothing the user still has to spend. Three cases cover the bound. The one gap: the setup report does not exist yet, so the text check runs on sample text and on the report lines; task 8.9 builds the real report and must pass it through `shareClaims`. `just check` at exit 0, `523 pass, 0 fail` across 42 files and `83 pass, 0 fail` across 10 lab files.

What 8.7 then measured is that the margin holds for ordinary growth and not for every request, so "keep below 100,000" is what this setting achieves for turn-by-turn growth and not a guarantee; the reading and its consequence are in `docs/window-weights.md`.
- [x] 8.7 Check the trigger of 90,000 against the rows of group 7 instead of deriving it. It is no longer an unsourced value: the Anthropic page `about-claude/models/optimizing-for-cost-and-intelligence` states that "In Anthropic's test with 10,000-token turns, a trigger of 90,000 cost 70% less per request than the default" (read 2026-10-28, `references.md`), which is the same value the archived code holds. What stays open is whether the 10,000-token turn size of that test matches our turns, because the margin has to cover the largest growth of one Haiku call plus the write turn. Verify: `docs/window-weights.md` holds our own largest-call growth beside the vendor quote, and says whether 90,000 clears it (`W5-3`). The section "The Haiku Trigger against Our Own Growth" holds both, and the answer is that **90,000 does not clear it**. `claudekit/haiku-growth/main.ts` read 1,120 transcripts of this login on 2026-10-29, counted each response once by `message.id`, took the prompt as the three input counts together because the price step counts cache reads and writes, and differenced consecutive responses: 2,196 Haiku 5.5 responses, 1,377 steps, 53 of them over the 10,000-token margin, the largest step 411,764, the largest pair of steps in a row 414,059, and the largest Haiku prompt on this machine 436,909. The rows of group 7 could not answer it, because `window.rows` is empty here, so the transcripts are the source and the doc says so. Two corrections were made before the figures were kept. **The first reading differenced by file, not by thread.** A transcript file interleaves the main thread with subagent sidechains, so two consecutive responses could belong to different threads and their difference was not the growth of one call. The thread of a response is now the last link reached by following `parentUuid` through the file's own `uuid` map, because `isSidechain` alone cannot tell two sidechains of one file apart. On the same 1,120 files the old form gives 1,383 steps with 58 over the margin against 1,377 with 53, so the fix removes 5 false steps and the largest step, which is inside one thread, is unchanged. **The cause was not what the figures suggested.** Each of the five largest steps is one request with `input_tokens` of 2 and almost the whole prompt in `cache_creation_input_tokens`, the largest being a prompt of 425,365 one response after a prompt of 13,601. So the margin is eaten by single requests whose prompt jumps because one large payload entered the context, not by turn-by-turn growth, and no trigger below the price step can prevent such a request, because the payload arrives inside one call. The value stays at 90,000, since no reading gives another and G10 bars picking one; what changes is the claim, which is now that the trigger lowers the share of requests priced above the step rather than ruling them out.

In window points both arms moved the five-hour window by `0` in all six runs**, so that half is below the resolution of the instrument rather than unmeasured: one point is a tenth of a percent and a compaction of a 23,000-token context does not reach it. The steadier comparison is the whole run, because it does not depend on where inside a step the boundary fell: over six identical steps with one compaction each the engine arm billed a mean of `0.8055` and the handler arm `0.1873`, a fall of 77%, and the handler arm reproduced to four decimals at `0.1791` and `0.1792` while the two engine runs differ by 18%. All of it is written into `docs/window-weights.md`, "The Price of the Handler, Measured", with the runs it came from. **The switch still stays off, and now for measured reasons rather than for the absence of a figure**, which the page states as three limits: the scale is wrong by about six times, since these compactions compacted about 23,000 tokens against the shipped 140,000 and the summarizer reads what it compacts; nothing here checks the summary, and the handler's step ended at about 10,000 tokens against the engine's 23,000, so part of the difference is a shorter summary and a summary that drops what the work needed is not a saving; and three runs per arm is the smoke floor and not the citable 10, so the direction is the same in all six runs while the size of it is not pinned. **Three defects of the instrument were found by running it, each fixed with a test that failed first.** Its `DEFAULT_THRESHOLD` was a fixed 30,000 tokens that its own fixture could never reach, because the context of a first step is almost all the plugin's own prompt: the first paid run started at 22,622 tokens, climbed through 23,767, 24,902, 26,047, and 27,182 to 28,319 over six steps, crossed nothing, and billed `0.28` for no price. And the marked row does not hold the window the compaction left, which the runs disproved rather than argued: two engine runs ended their marked step at 23,423 and 23,086 tokens against the 22,710 and 22,718 of the row before, so the context had risen, and those two runs read `0.0785` and `0.0892` against the `0.0237` of the run whose step ended at its boundary. `Price` therefore carries `fell`, and `priceLine` says "at most" for a run where the context did not fall, so a reader is told which of the two figures they hold; the claim that the row "carries the window the compaction left" is corrected in the JSDoc of `window.ts` and in `docs/window-weights.md`. The five rules map onto them: the cadence has two, that a check runs at every sixth step and at no other, which drives steps 1 to 5 for no request and then 6 to 12 for two, and that the first step of a turn never checks; the sub-agent rule has one, a step carrying an `agentId` passed through with no check; the two lists have three, a line on the shown list dropped whatever its case and final period, the same for the known list, and a new line shown and joined to the shown list; the back-off has three, an ignored offer past the free count leaving a skip that grows while one inside the free count leaves none, and a skipped check spending one skip so that the next runs; the open-surface rule has two, no check while an offer is open and none on a turn that already offered, which is the pair the original gates on (`docs/keep/plugins/you-should-know-rebuild.txt`, `HOW IT TRIGGERS`), with a third beside them for a session whose surfaces draw no band at all. Two further cases cover the clearing of a stale offer after two typed prompts and the closing of an open offer when the session ends, so no row is left open across a boundary.

So the first four cases pin the installed default, which is the switch that matters: `settingsOf({})` is off, `keptSettings()` is off before `register` runs, only `true` turns it on while `"true"`, `1`, and `false` do not, and the shipped `plugins/claudetto-you-should-know/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` declares `enabled` as `"type": "boolean"` with `"default": false`. The fifth records that an off plugin still resolves `claude-haiku-5-5` at `medium`, so turning it on prices nothing beyond the call itself. The last two cover the task's own words: no field of any row matches `/observer|enabled|shouldKnow/i`, and every row read as this plugin's options leaves it off, with the row reduced to its scalar fields by `asOptions`, which is the shape a settings file can carry. Proven non-vacuous both ways rather than asserted: planting `"default": true` in the manifest failed exactly one case, `the shipped manifest defaults it to off`, giving `6 pass, 1 fail`; planting `enabled: true` into `SHARED` of `plugins/claudetto/src/tiers.ts` failed exactly the two tier cases, giving `5 pass, 2 fail`; both plants were then removed and the file is back to `7 pass`. ## 13. Structure

The counts and the per-file detail of this group are in `survey.md`, "Files as Directories" and "Test Layout", and the decisions are in `decisions.md`. A cluster is sibling flat modules sharing a name prefix that should be one folder, such as `automode.ts` with `automode-environment.ts` and `automode-faults.ts`. The order is cheapest first by the count of importing files outside the cluster, so an early task proves the method on one importer before `tiers/` moves with twelve.

One hazard applies to every move of this group and is recorded once here. The installed `0.4.0-alpha.2` carries `modules/work/protect/`, whose `UNDONE` text reverts a file that a `Bash` command changed, and a `mv` creates the destination, so the revert deletes it while `mv` has already removed the source. That cost two files on 2026-10-29 before they were recovered from a scratch copy. Until the installed plugin is the rebuilt one, each move in this group writes the destination with `Write` and removes the source separately, rather than calling `mv`. The live tree has no such slice, so `0.0.0` is not affected: `grep` for that text across `plugins/` returns nothing and the only `protect`-named file, `plugins/claudetto/src/gate/protected-findings.ts`, holds no `writeFile`, `rename`, or `rm`.

After the fix the file gives `3 pass, 0 fail` and `just check` exits 0 as one command: `Checked 301 files` with no fix applied from Biome, `0 issues in 0 files` from `markdownlint-cli2` over 68 files, `✓ No type errors in 288 files`, `825 pass, 0 fail`, which is the previous 822 plus these 3, three `✔ Validation passed`, and `114 pass, 0 fail` with `29 pass, 0 fail` from the two labs. It refuses three shapes rather than the one the task names, because the convention was landed in three forms and a check for one of them would pass a tree that breaks the other two. The prefix shape is the one named here, a stem that is a sibling's stem plus a tail. The suffix shape is two or more siblings sharing a final `-` segment with no bare file carrying it, which is the case `survey.md` calls a suffix cluster and resolved as `gate/findings/`; planting `tools/alpha-pipe.ts` and `tools/beta-pipe.ts` makes it exit 1 naming both and the tail. The repeat shape is a stem starting with its own folder's name and a `-`, where the folder already says the word; planting `tools/evals/evals-extra.ts` makes it exit 1. A `__tests__/` is read as belonging to the folder above it, because a test is named after what it covers and the concern it sits in is the one holding the sources, so the rules reach a test name too. **The check found two live faults on the tree, and both were fixed rather than excused.** The first was the only cluster left in the plugin: `plugins/claudetto/src/run-limits/` held `spawn.ts` beside `spawn-hook.ts`, which is the prefix shape, and `spawn-hook.ts`, `failure-hook.ts`, and `stop-hook.ts`, which is the suffix shape. The importers are named on both sides, which is the first verify clause: `claudekit/compaction-price/price.ts` exports the interface `Price`, `priceOf`, and `priceLine`, and is read by `claudekit/compaction-price/main.ts` and by `tests/claudekit/compaction-price/price.test.ts` and by nothing else; `claudekit/compaction-cost/price.ts` exports `OPUS`, `money`, the interface `Mix`, `mixOf`, `mean`, and `meanPrompt`, and is read by five modules inside `compaction-cost/` and by nothing outside it. **The deciding fact is that no module imports both and the two share no export name**, so there is no shared code a merge would extract and nothing that would have to pick between them; what a merge would produce is one folder holding two unrelated `price.ts` under new names. Two further facts point the same way. The tools measure different things from different inputs and only one can spend money, since `just compaction-price` prices one compaction from the recorder's rows and makes paid calls with `--run` while `just compaction-cost` replays transcripts off disk, free and with no model call at all. And they are coupled to different things: `claudekit/compaction-price/main.ts` imports four probe modules and takes `rowsOf` from the seam at `claudekit/plugin.ts` because it starts `claude` and reads its event stream, while `claudekit/compaction-cost/` imports no probe module and no plugin module, so folding the first into the second would pull the probe transport into a tool that never starts a session. One observation is recorded rather than acted on, so that it is a choice and not an oversight: `claudekit/compaction-cost/price.ts` exports `money`, `mean`, `meanPrompt`, and the `OPUS` price row, which makes it a money-and-means helper rather than a pricer, and `money.ts` would name it better; that rename is outside task 14.7, whose clause is about two files of one tool, and outside this decision, which is about the boundary between two tools. `bun tools/thin-claims.ts` exits 0 over the new section.

## 15. Comments

`survey.md`, "Comment Audit", holds the counts: 190 shipped files, 2,360 JSDoc lines, 950 `//` lines, no other block comments, and 257 of 963 exported declarations with no JSDoc block above them, split 150 in `plugins/`, 42 in `tools/`, and 65 in `claudekit/`, which task 14.2 renamed from `dev/` after this count was taken. The rule the user set is that a public API surface carries JSDoc and a non-doc comment survives only where a "why" warrants it.

- [x] 15.1 Write the comment rule down and build the checker that holds it, because 257 gaps cannot be closed by review alone. The rule has two halves: every exported declaration of a shipped source carries a JSDoc block directly above it, and a comment that only restates the declaration below it is a defect rather than documentation. Verify: the checker exits 1 on a planted export with no JSDoc and names its file and line, exits 1 on a planted comment that repeats the name below it, and exits 0 once both are fixed. The rule is written in `decisions.md` under "The Comment Rule", in three parts, and the checker is `tools/jsdoc.ts` at 246 lines with `tools/__tests__/jsdoc.spec.ts` at 13 tests. **The rule's third part is that it stops at two halves and says why.** A non-doc comment stays only where it gives a reason the code cannot show, and that part is not checked by a command and is not going to be, because the question it asks is whether a sentence is a reason and a word list cannot answer it; the evidence is `tools/no-narration.ts`'s own seven-entry allowlist, which exists because its word patterns fire on dated readings that are evidence rather than narration. The same evidence sets how hard the second half may push: a block is refused only when **every** content word of it appears in the name, so one word the name does not hold is enough to pass, because a check that teaches a session to reword in place of fixing is worse than no check. Two scope decisions are recorded with their reasons. A shipped source is one of the `CHECKED_GLOBS` of `tools/file-lines.ts` less every test file, taken through its `isTestFile`, so a spec and a planted fixture are out of scope. And a backticked span counts as no word, because a code item a block names is almost always the declaration's own name or one of its parameters and both are on the line below; that choice is what makes `/** The limit of `path`. */` above `limitOf` a restatement, which it is, since a parameter name echoed from the signature is not a fact. **All three verify clauses were proved by running the CLI and not by reading it.** Planting `tools/planted.ts` holding one bare export and one restating block made `bun tools/jsdoc.ts` print `tools/planted.ts:1: exported const plantedValue has no JSDoc block directly above it` and `tools/planted.ts:4: the JSDoc above PlantedThing uses only words of its own name, so it adds no fact`, so both halves name the file and the line; the file was then removed. ## 17. Prose Checks in `tools/`

The user read `tools/` on 2026-10-29 and called out the checks that are word lists over prose rather than readings of behavior, naming `no-narration` as the example. The repo already holds the standard that settles it, in `~/.claude/rules/claudetto-tests.md`: "Delete each test that checks the prose of the implementation instead of behavior. Prose is the wording of a prompt, a message, or a doc." That rule is written about a test, and the same reason reaches a check in `just check`, because a gate on wording stops correct work and teaches a session to reword rather than to fix.

The three candidates and what each is, read on 2026-10-29. `tools/no-narration.ts` is 129 lines over three regular expressions at `:18-33` that match the words `task`, `group`, `in this session`, `the user confirmed`, `archived`, and `ported unchanged` in a comment, and it runs in `just check` at `justfile:16`. Its own 7-entry `ALLOWED` allowlist at `:39-69` is the evidence against it: six shipped files and one test line are exempted by path because the regexes fire on dated readings that are evidence rather than narration, so the check cannot tell its target from its opposite and needs a hand-kept list to stay green. `tools/thin-claims.ts` is 89 lines over a `NUMBER_WORDS` list at `:8` and a `RUN_COUNT` expression at `:24`, and it runs at `justfile:24`; unlike the first it also checks that a cited line exists, which is a reading of the tree and not of wording. `tools/openings/evidence.ts` is a signal that `docs/prompt/footprint.md:314` itself records at 89% false positives and in no hook, and no `justfile` recipe runs it.

## 18. Files as Directories in `docs/`

The user read `docs/` on 2026-10-29 and found the same files-as-directories shape that group 13 fixed in the source, and asked for it fixed everywhere except `docs/keep/`, which is a decision-page folder with its own naming. A `find` over `docs/` at depth 1 on that date gives 58 Markdown files beside the `keep/` folder, and three prefixes carry most of them: 28 files begin `evidence-`, 12 begin `dossier-`, and 4 begin `prompt-`, so 44 of the 58 are in a cluster that a folder would name once.

Two shapes inside those clusters are worse than the prefix alone. Five files are numbered siblings of another file, which is exactly the `foo-bar2.ts` shape the user named: `dossier-exports-2.md` and `dossier-exports-3.md` beside `dossier-exports.md`, `dossier-latest-2.md` beside `dossier-latest.md`, `evidence-handoff-cost-2.md` beside `evidence-handoff-cost.md`, and `evidence-usage-evidence-2.md` beside `evidence-usage-evidence.md`. Three more are lettered siblings, `dossier-sep-a.md`, `dossier-sep-b.md`, and `dossier-sep-c.md`. And one cluster nests inside another: six files begin `evidence-evals`, which is a folder inside a folder written as a prefix inside a prefix.

The hazard that governs every task of this group: no `Bash` command may move or write a file under `docs/`, because the installed claudetto `0.4.0-alpha.2` carries `modules/work/protect/`, which reverts a file a `Bash` command changed, and reverting a created file deletes it. That destroyed two files earlier on 2026-10-29. Use the `Read`, `Write`, and `Edit` tools, which are not guarded.

## 19. Multiline Literals Over Escaped Newlines

The user asked on 2026-10-29 for a multiline string literal wherever a newline is escaped by hand or assembled by a call, over the `"\n"` and `join("\n")` shapes. A grep over `plugins/`, `tools/`, `claudekit/`, and `tests/` on that date counts 43 `join("\n")` calls across 32 files, and 87 lines under `plugins/`, `tools/`, and `dev/` hold a double-quoted literal with an escaped `\n` inside it. The reason to prefer the literal is that the text then reads on the page the way it reads in the output, so a reader sees the blank line and the indentation that a `\n` hides, which matters most for the prompt and message text this tree ships.

The two shapes are not the same job and must not be converted by one rule. A `join("\n")` over a built array is correct and must stay, because its parts come from a loop or a filter and no literal can hold them; the ones to convert are those joining a literal array written in place. An escaped `\n` inside a short literal, such as a separator passed to a split, is also correct and must stay.

- [x] 19.1 Write the rule with both exceptions and build the checker, before changing any text, because 130 sites cannot be judged by review alone. Verify: the checker exits 1 on a planted `join("\n")` over an array literal written in place and on a planted multi-line message built from escaped newlines, exits 0 on a `join("\n")` over a mapped or filtered value, and exits 0 on an escaped `\n` used as a separator argument. `tools/literals.ts` at 298 lines, with `tools/__tests__/literals.spec.ts` at `27 pass, 0 fail`, which covers all four cases of the clause and each boundary below. It walks `CHECKED_GLOBS` of `tools/file-lines.ts` and it lexes rather than greps, so a `\n` or a `.join` inside a comment, a template text, or a regex body is never seen. The group text names `dev/` as a root; `dev/` does not exist, so the scope is `plugins/`, `tools/`, `claudekit/`, and `tests/`. **The message boundary, measured rather than chosen.** A double-quoted literal is a message when a `\n` lies between its first and its last character that is not a `\n`; a `\n` at either end is a terminator or a needle and stays. Of the 184 double-quoted literals in the tree holding the text `\n`, 43 are messages, 99 are the whole literal `"\n"`, and 42 carry newlines at an end only, such as `"x\n"` and `"\n---"`, so 141 of 184 stay. The brief proposed "text on both sides of the `\n`", which is wider: it also catches `"\nbody\n"`, which is a block with its own terminators and not a hidden shape. **The join boundary.** A `join("\n")` is a fault when the receiver literally ends in `]` and no top-level element is a spread, because a variable or a call never ends in `]`. An index such as `groups[0].join` is excluded and `return [...]` is read as a literal. **The reading, 54 faults, which corrects the group text.** The 2026-10-29 grep counted 43 `join("\n")` calls and 87 escaped-newline lines, and both overstate the work: only 11 joins are over an array literal written in place, the rest being over mapped, filtered, or pushed values that no literal can hold. Per root: `tools/` 21, `tests/` 18, `claudekit/` 8, `plugins/` 7. Only 2 of the 54 are in shipped code, `plugins/claudetto/src/handoff.ts:275` and `read-back.ts:31`, both joins; the other 5 under `plugins/` are messages in tests. One of the 184 literals is a JSON blob in `plugins/claudetto/src/secrets/gitleaks/rules.ts` whose `\\n` is an escaped backslash and not a newline, and the end-only rule already leaves it alone. **Five shapes the check deliberately cannot see**, each a miss rather than a false positive, because a check that cries wolf gets suppressed and this repo bans suppression comments: a `\n` in a single-quoted literal, a `\n` in the text of a template literal, parts pushed into a variable before the join, a `"a\n" + "b"` concatenation, and a regex literal following `)` or an identifier, of which the tree holds none. One shape it over-reports is a literal array with a conditional element, which tasks 19.2 and 19.3 have to judge by hand where it appears. `bun tools/jsdoc.ts`, `bun tools/no-narration.ts`, and `bun tools/file-lines.ts` all at exit 0, and `bunx biome check` clean on both files.

## 20. The Agent's Own Budget: Context, Five-Hour, and Seven-Day

The user asked on 2026-10-29 whether the restart is aware of their five-hour and weekly usage, and then said what it is for: the agent should see the context window left, the five-hour share, and the seven-day share, "so the agent can adjust accordingly". Measured against the tree, none of the three reaches the agent, and one of them is not even read.

What the recorder does today. It reads one window: `plugins/claudetto/src/window/window.ts:20` sets `FIVE_HOUR = "five_hour"` and `:112` is `input.rateLimits.find((limit) => limit.kind === FIVE_HOUR)`, so a `seven_day` reading arriving in the same `rateLimits` array is discarded. `WindowRow` carries `window` at `:55`, `percent` at `:56`, `points` at `:58`, and `usd` at `:60`, with no seven-day field, so no row can price a weekly share even in hindsight. Every figure the report prints names the one window in words, at `plugins/claudetto/src/window/report.ts:41`, `:87`, and `:103`, each ending "points of the five-hour window".

Where the rows go, which is the half the user's question turns on. They go to the store and to `$.ui.log`, at `plugins/claudetto/src/window/hooks.ts:68` and `:136`, and a UI log is not the agent's context. The one place in the whole plugin that puts text where the agent reads it is `onStop` of `plugins/claudetto/src/run-limits/hooks.ts`, at `:123`, which adds to `additionalContext`, and it reports in-flight agents rather than any budget. So the agent currently adjusts to nothing: it cannot see the context left, the five-hour share it is spending, or the seven-day share at all.

The tree already depends on the seven-day window in three places, which makes this a gap and not a new feature. The comment at `window.ts:23` names `seven_day` as "the longest window the engine reports, beside `five_hour` and a gateway's `spend_limit`", and uses it only to size `KEEP_MS` at seven days. `shareClaims` at `plugins/claudetto/src/window/report.ts:113` finds a claim like a share of a weekly limit inside a text, so the tree polices a weekly claim it cannot measure. And `AGENTS.md` carries the standing rule "Give the expected cost and the current weekly share with the question", which no code supports: that arithmetic lives in prose at `docs/window-weights.md:55`, and tasks 3.10 and 10.5 each did the multiplication by hand to size a run.

One constraint the group must respect rather than discover: text put in front of the agent every turn is paid for on every turn, and this tree holds a measured budget discipline, with `plugins/claudetto/agents/main.md` bounded at 5,680 request JSON characters and the shipped request cut from 42,687 characters to 27,403. A budget line is worth nothing if it costs more than it saves, so task 20.2 prices itself.

Making `points` optional is the change that propagates, so `isRow` accepts a row carrying either field and `perTask`, `weightOf`, and the line builders skip a row that lacks the field they read. `bun test plugins/claudetto/src plugins/claudetto/tests tests/claudetto` at `400 pass, 0 fail` across 26 files. **One consumer outside the plugin broke and I fixed it**, which no verify clause asked for: `claudekit/compaction-price/price.ts` read `row.points` as a required number at three places and stopped type-checking. No paid round was run: the guard and the arithmetic are both testable from planted values, so neither needed a model call.

## 21. The Cuts Inventory against a Capture

Found on 2026-10-29 while task 16.3 priced its own delegation line, and the rows of `plugins/claudetto/src/cuts.ts` are corrected in that same tick. The inventory had drifted 1,382 characters from the request it describes: `SHIPPED_TOTAL` read 29,080 where `just capture --shipped` reads 30,462. Four rows were wrong and one piece had no row at all.

What the corrections were, each from a capture of that date. `tool texts` moved 3,118 to 2,999, and it is the one row no isolating capture reaches, because hiding our tool texts means hiding the plugin, so it is the residual of the reading once every other piece is measured; the `tools` array of the same pair of runs fell 16,021 to 13,021, which corroborates it to within a character. `main agent body` moved 16 to 27, the difference between the default body at 5,680 request characters and `agents/main.md` at 5,653. `agent listing` moved 0 to -492, because the row was written while `main` was the only agent file and `src/register.ts` refuses that one offer, while `Artemis` and `Athena` are delegation targets that the harness lists: 30,462 with both files present against 29,970 with both moved aside. `output style` moved -1,693 to -1,705, measured as 30,462 against 28,757 with the folder moved aside. The missing piece is the handoff context block of `src/handoff.ts`, a `<system-reminder>` of 733 characters in the first user message costing 770 on the wire, which is now the row `handoff context`; re-serializing the captured body without that content part gives 29,680 against 30,450.

The instrument has run-to-run variance of 12 characters, read as 30,462 and 30,450 for the same arm, which is why the residual row carries it rather than a row claiming a precision the capture does not have.

That correction is written into the constant's own JSDoc instead of left in this list. **One seam moved:** `capture` and `runArm` are now `tools/capture/arm.ts`, which `run.ts` and `inventory.ts` both read, because a reading is comparable between two commands only while both take it the same way. **A defect of `--dump` was fixed on the way:** `capture` wrote `JSON.stringify(first, undefined, 2)` where `first` is already the body's text, so a dumped body arrived as one escaped line rather than as indented JSON; it now re-indents through `JSON.parse`. **The 12 characters were then chased to their source with four more isolating captures, which corrected three figures of the inventory's own pages.** The whole fall sits in `tools`, 13,021 to 13,009, and the arm with `Artemis.md` and `Athena.md` moved aside is unchanged at 13,009, so the 12 were the roster's contribution to the `ListAgents` description and the roster no longer reaches it. The 12 may be taken by one row only, and `cuts.ts:49` gives it to the `tool texts` residual, which is a `tools` figure: that row now reads `chars: 3011`, and `bun -e` over `16021 - 13009` prints `3012` for the same cut, so the corroboration the row claims holds to a character, where giving the 12 to the agent listing instead would have put it 13 out. ## 22. Claude Code's Own Surface, and the Two Capabilities the Restart Dropped by Accident

Opened on 2026-10-30 by `just inventory` exiting 1, and widened by the user reading the skills-listing table of `docs/surfaces.md`: "we want none of claude code's builtin skills of any kind", then "all built-in skills and plugins get disabled". Chasing the second of those found that the pre-restart tree already wrote both switches (`docs/surfaces.md:496`) and the restart's `tierSnippet` wrote neither, so the largest remaining lever was a regression and not a discovery. Reading what the switches remove then found two capabilities that the restart dropped with no record: `setup`, which `README.md:33` tells the user to run after an install, and `dedupe`. Two gates in `just check` were vacuous and are why nobody noticed.

- [x] 22.1 Repair the drift that `just inventory` reports, by re-reading each stale row rather than by moving the recorded figure to match. Verify: every row of `just inventory` reads its recorded value, each re-recorded constant names the capture set it came from, and the cause of the drift is placed rather than plugged. **The 192 characters are the engine's and not this tree's.** `baseline` read 42,879 against a recorded 42,687 and `deny list` read 30,713 against 30,521, each stale by exactly 192, and the `plain` arm loads no plugin of ours at all. The third `system` block of that arm grew from 5,628 to 5,818 characters under an unchanged `2.1.312`, and those 190 raw characters cost 192 on the wire because `scoreOf` of `tools/capture/score.ts` measures `system` as the joined block texts without JSON punctuation and `total` as the raw body, so the two characters are the escaping of the 190. Nothing else could carry it: `tools` held at 28,187, `messages` at 8,061, and the working-folder path is 34 characters in both runs because `tools/capture/arm.ts` uses a fixed prefix. That is also why `plugins/claudetto/src/deny.ts` changed in its totals only and in none of its `chars` or `tools` figures. Re-recorded, each from a `just capture --arm plain` run of 2026-10-30: `BASELINE_TOTAL` 42,687 to 42,879, the three single-deny totals 37,331 to 37,523, 38,055 to 38,247 and 40,509 to 40,701, `DENIED_TOGETHER_TOTAL` 30,521 to 30,713, the `ToolSearch` row of `NEVER_DENY` 31,532 to 31,741, and both rows of `DROPPED_DENIES` to 42,879 and 42,867. `BASELINE_TOOLS` 28,187 and `DENIED_TOGETHER_TOOLS` 16,021 are unchanged, as is every per-tool `chars`. Cost: about thirteen capture runs, each a `claude -p` against a fake Messages API on `127.0.0.1`, so no provider was reached and no model call was billed.
- [x] 22.2 Turn off Claude Code's bundled skills and its built-in plugins, which the pre-restart snippet did and this tree does not, and measure the result rather than assuming the direction. Verify: the shipped settings carry both keys, each key is pinned to the installed program, the figure is read from a capture, and a check names the capability that the switch would cost. **`disableBundledSkills` alone raises the first request by 11,379 characters**, 42,879 to 54,258, because the `Workflow` definition inlines the workflow guidance that the skill listing no longer carries: `tools` goes 28,187 to 45,142 while `messages` falls 8,061 to 2,485. That is the same swell `NEVER_DENY` already records for denying `Skill`, reached by a second route, so the key is a cut only once `Workflow` is denied. With the deny list in place the growth has nowhere to land, `tools` is byte-identical at 16,021 in both arms, and the whole fall is kept: 30,713 against 25,137. In the shipped arm the two switches together take 30,291 to 24,378, and **every character of the 5,913 leaves `messages`**, 11,029 to 5,116, with `tools` holding at 12,850 and `system` at 5,708, so the new row of `cuts.ts` takes nothing another row also counts. `plugins/claudetto/src/builtins.ts` holds both keys with their pins, `disableBundledSkills` at `chunk-ey5hx8vw.js:146457` and `enabledPlugins` at `:160442`, the thirteen built-in plugin names, and the six readings as `BUILTIN_CUTS`. **Twelve of the thirteen are turned off and `sec-default` is kept**, because it declares `enabledFromTrustedSettingsOnly` and `enabledFromPolicyOnly` and so a user `false` does not decide it (`docs/gates.md:100`); every kept row carries its reason and a test asserts that it does. The id form is `cc-plugin-<name>@builtin`, read from the short-to-full name map at `chunk-ey5hx8vw.js:13093` and corroborated by the literal `cc-plugin-sec-default@builtin` at `chunk-0gsajprh.js:11133`; my first draft of `builtinId` omitted the `cc-plugin-` prefix and `tools/capture/__tests__/capture.spec.ts` caught it, while the captures themselves had used the correct form, so the readings stand. **The capability question was settled by a free probe before the switch shipped**, because the `plugin` arm of the capture lists no plugin skills at all: the restart plugin shipped none, so that arm could not answer whether a skill of ours survives. Probing the installed `0.4.0-alpha.2` against the same fake API showed `claudetto:dedupe` and `claudetto:find-docs` listed in both arms while every bundled name left, with the same 5,576 fall, and three independent captures agree on it. **The chunk name is content-addressed and moves inside one version**, answering from `chunk-ce589w22.js` on 2026-10-29 and `chunk-ey5hx8vw.js` on 2026-10-30 under an unchanged `2.1.312`, so the pin test asserts that the installed program still holds the key's name and not the module or the offset; that is written into the header of `plugins/claudetto/src/tiers/keys.ts`, where `TIER_KEYS` grows from six pins to eight.

The default target is `~/.claude/settings.json` and the `justfile` recipe is `just setup`. `docs/keep/skills/setup.txt:17` now reads `drop as a skill` with a `DECISION WHY` naming the CLI, and `architecture.md:15` says CLI rather than skill.

The skill is at `plugins/claudetto/skills/dedupe/SKILL.md` with a 138-character `description:` line, and it runs `bunx jscpd --no-tips <path>...` with explicit paths. **Its listing costs 229 characters, read and not computed**: 24,378 without it against 24,607 with it, minutes apart, where counting the name, the description, and the markup around them predicts 171. So the row of `cuts.ts` is a cost with a negative sign. `bunx jscpd` errored before this with a missing `dev` path, because `dev/` was deleted in the restart and `.jscpd.json` still listed it; the path list is now `plugins`, `tests`, `tools`, `claudekit`.

Verify: each gate fails on the tree as it was before 22.3 and 22.4, and passes on the tree as it ships. **`tools/skill-flags.ts` was vacuous: zero skills gave zero faults**, so a tree that ships no skill at all satisfied the skill gate. It now reads which pages ship, a page shipping when `SET: ours` holds and the first word of `USER DECISION`, or of `RECOMMENDATION` when there is no decision, is `keep` or `name-only`, and a tree missing a shipping skill is a fault. **`tools/citations.ts` checked Markdown under `docs/` alone**, so the 99 keep pages with their `.txt` extension were never checked, and about seventy of them carried dead pre-restart paths; `PAGE_GLOBS` now covers the keep pages too and the pages are repaired, with paths that only exist in the deleted tree prefixed `archive/`. `bun tools/citations.ts` and `bun tools/keep-pages.ts` both exit 0. Two limits of the repaired gate are worth stating rather than hiding: it reads a citation only inside backticks, so a dead path written as bare prose still passes, which holds today for lines of `plugins/claudetto/src/register.ts`, of `plugins/claudetto/src/prompts.ts`, and of `plugins/claudetto/agents/Apollo.md`; and `.jscpd-baseline.json` is now stale at 31 new clones, deliberately not re-baselined because re-baselining would accept 31 real clones silently.
